judging the predicate](global/predicate-fires-after-gripper-retreat.md) \\u2014 you released a held object into/onto a target and libero_terminated is still false\\n- [Retreat the eef fully clear of a placement before concluding the predicate failed](global/predicate-gated-by-eef-proximity-retreat-clear.md) \\u2014 after a geometrically-correct place, libero_terminated stays false for many steps while the gripper hovers over the just-placed object\\n- [Recenter after pre-grasp yaw before trusting Pi0.](global/pregrasp-yaw-recenter.md) \\u2014 A pre-grasp wrist yaw is used to change a bowl or cup rim-hook geometry, and the rotation shifts the wrist or EEF away from the visually selected target\\n- [Find a container's true floor by descend-until-OSC-stall and read the stall height](global/probe-container-floor-by-stall-height.md) \\u2014 you must place a held object on the floor of a container (basket/drawer/box) whose interior floor you cannot see from above (occluded by rim/liner/other objects) and depth/segment centroids are rim-biased\\n- [Anchor duplicate-object wrist refinement to a visible relation landmark.](global/relation-anchored-wrist-refine.md) \\u2014 A task chooses one of multiple identical objects by a spatial relation, and wrist views can contain the wrong duplicate.\\n- [Select identical targets by the named spatial relation before wrist refinement](global/relation-selected-identical-object.md) \\u2014 A task contains duplicate or visually identical objects and names a relation such as on, left of, right of, between, or next to\\n- [Remeasure a held payload after every loaded servo before descending](global/remeasure-loaded-offset-before-descent.md) \\u2014 a large asymmetric object is carried by a handle and precise support placement depends on body-to-EEF offset\\n- [After releasing into a lined basket, retreat straight up before moving laterally](global/retreat-high-before-lateral-near-lined-basket.md) \\u2014 placing items into a woven/cloth-lined basket or any container with a soft rim/liner that stands above the rigid rim; especially multi-item drop-ins where you must traverse away between placements\\n- [Clear a released wrist by reversing the corridor that successfully entered.](global/reverse-entry-corridor-clearance.md) \\u2014 A released object is cavity-side but axial or vertical wrist retreat re-hooks or extracts it.\\n- [Test perimeter contact zones before rejecting a fixture-relative placement.](global/right-front-perimeter-contact.md) \\u2014 A fixture-relative goal says front or side and central visual bands fail despite clean object placement\\n- [Use a capped contact skill to seat rim-perched objects into containers.](global/rim-perch-contact-seat.md) \\u2014 An object has been released at a rimmed container and is visibly perched on the lip or liner while the In predicate is still false.\\n- [A large rotate_wrist yaw drifts the eef ~0.1-0.15m; re-center before descending](global/rotate-wrist-90deg-drifts-eef-recenter-after.md) \\u2014 you rotate_wrist by a large yaw (e.g. 90deg) to align fingers, then need a precise xy over a target\\n- [Disambiguate look-alike cans by RGB label, never by SAM3 brand-noun prompt](global/sam3-brand-noun-can-collision.md) \\u2014 two or more similar cans/cylinders are present and the task names one by brand (\\\"tomato sauce\\\", \\\"alphabet soup\\\", a soup can)\\n- [Re-issue a pick with a nearby landmark when generic picking makes only contact.](global/spatial-qualified-repick.md) \\u2014 A generic Pi0 pick contacts the target object but leaves it upright or ungrasped, and a distinctive nearby landmark is visible in the scene.\\n- [Support an elongated held object on the destination floor before chaining yaw and pitch rotations](global/support-assisted-axis-reorientation.md) \\u2014 an elongated held object must be reoriented inside a shallow container and airborne wrist rotations slip, drift, or exceed the reachable workspace\\n- [Translate a weak hooked grasp with surface support before lifting over thresholds.](global/support-guided-weak-hook-translation.md) \\u2014 A narrow rim or handle hook survives gentle motion but slips under airborne rotation or long carry.\\n- [Re-check duplicate identity before tuning a non-firing placement.](global/verify-duplicate-semantics.md) Both \"tomato sauce can\" and \"alphabet soup can\" matched the single\nmost can-like object (highest objectness), regardless of which brand was on it.\nThe score was even high (0.77-0.90) for the wrong prompt, so score is not a guard.\n\n**How to apply:** Decide can identity in the agentview hi-res RGB by LABEL colour\nand imagery, not by the SAM3 label: e.g. tomato_sauce = red/tomato label,\nalphabet_soup = blue label. Releasing from z~0.20 while horizontally over the liner avoided the rim contact state and terminated immediately.\n**How to apply:** Identify the container semantically in agentview, use wrist to align over the interior liner/cavity, keep gripper closed during carry, and release from a high over-cavity pose around 4-6 cm above the rim rather than forcing a low descent. For low object-frame basket tasks, z~0.19-0.21 worked here.\n**Falsify:** If a high over-cavity release repeatedly bounces the object out while a lower straight-down placement terminates, this lesson should be narrowed to this basket/box geometry.\n**Related:** [[task-family_libero_object_task_t0]]\n"

So the can is lying with its axis roughly along the image horizontal = ±y direction! The label wraps around the visible side; the can's circular ends would be left and right. Actually looking at the shape: it's a cylinder seen from the side; the top edge is a curve; the ends are at the left and right. Hmm, but I see a curved edge at the top-right which might be the rim of the can's mouth (the opening is visible at the top-right as a thin ellipse). That suggests the can's axis points up-right = +x/-y.

Either way, the fingers close along y, and the can's body is between the fingers.

The can's image center is at about (row 700, col 510) — nicely centered horizontally, slightly below center. The fingers close at cols ~200 and ~830 — so the can is between them ✓.

Let me estimate the offset: the can's center at col 510 vs the image center 512 → dy ≈ 0. Row 700 vs 512 → +188 px down → -x.

Hmm, so the can is ~188px below the camera's center → the can is at a smaller x than the eef. How much? Scale: the can's height (7.4cm) appears as ~400px (rows 490-895) → 54 px/cm. So 188px = 3.5cm. So the can is at x ≈ -0.148 - 0.035 = -0.183?? Hmm, that's 3.5cm more in -x than the eef.

But wait, this is the projection of a point 0.1m below the camera — and it's a 3D object, so care is needed. The can's TOP surface is closer to the camera; the can's center projection...

Hmm. Let me reconsider: the depth. The can's top surface z ≈ 0.095; the camera at z 0.108. So the camera is only 1.3cm above the can's top?! Then the can should appear huge/very close. Indeed the can appears large (400px for 7.4cm → 54px/cm → fov = 1024/54 = 19cm at the can's distance). If the camera-to-can-top distance were 1.3cm, the fov would be ~2cm. So the can's top can't be at 0.095 with the camera at 0.108.

So the can's actual top must be well below: let's use the fov = 19cm at fov/2 ... the distance d satisfies fov_width ≈ 2*d*tan(39°) = 1.62*d. If the fov = 19cm → d = 0.117m. So the can's top surface is ~0.117m below the camera → z ≈ 0.108 - 0.117 = -0.01?! Negative. Contradiction.

Something's off: maybe the can is lying further away and appears smaller than I estimated. Let's re-measure: The can in the wrist image spans rows ~490-895 (405 px) and cols ~370-670 (300px). A lying can (7.4 long x 7.4 diameter) — the observed 405x300 px. If 7.4cm ≈ 400px → 54px/cm. Hmm.

Alternatively, the fov of the wrist camera might be narrower than my estimate (~65°). If the fov is 45°: fov_width = 0.83*d. At z=0.108, d≈0.1m → fov = 8.3cm → 123px/cm → the can would be 900px. Too big.

Let's calibrate the wrist scale empirically using the earlier step-12 measurement: at camera z 0.412 (eef), the basket (20cm) spanned 315px → 15.8px/cm, implying fov=65cm at that distance. The distance from the camera to the basket rim (z 0.11) = 0.30m. So fov=65cm at 0.30m distance → fov angle = 2*atan(32.5/30) = 94°. Very wide (fisheye-ish). Then at a distance d, px/cm = 1024/(2*d*tan(47°)) = 1024/(2.14*d[cm]).

At step 14: the can's top surface at z~0.095, camera z 0.108 → d = 1.3cm → px/cm = 1024/2.8 = 366 px/cm. Nonsense.

Clearly my assumption about the wrist camera's mounting (that it's at the eef position) is WRONG. The wrist camera is likely mounted at the wrist, offset back and up, tilted downward. So its actual XYZ differs a lot from the eef.

Given that, my wrist-derived world coords ARE computed by the tool using proper calibration (extrinsics), so they should be right — the tool knows the camera pose.
